1. General Information

1.1 Introduction

The Sepehr-Jufair Field situated in the Abadan Plain, Southwest of Khuzestan Province,
about 40 km West of Ahwaz. The Sepehr-Jufair  field covers about 352 square
kilometers area with approximately 23 kilometers long and 27 kilometers wide. Figure
1 illustrates the geographical situation of the Sepehr and Jufair gentle culminations that
are located in the vicinity of South Azadegan and Yadavaran giant fields.

                   Figure 1: Geographical location of the Sepehr-Jufair field.

              Figure 2: Location of Sepehr-Jufair Oil field – Side view of Structure

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

1.2 Well Information

      Well Name                            SPH-03

  Country / Province                          Iran / Khuzestan

        Area                           Abadan Plain

          Field                                  Sepehr-Jufair

      Well Class.                           Production well

      Well Type                                  Vertical well

      Well No.                               3

    Reference Well                           SPH-01

       Rig Name                                 Rig 27 Fath

    Well Objective                                        Original producer: Lower Fahliyan

                                     Alternative producer: Lower Gadvan

                                  Geodetic system: UTM39N, WGS84

                           Wellhead coordinates       X: 221896    Y: 3453048    Well Location

                            Position in respect with reference well (SPH‐01): N 1 W

        GLE                                     7.9 m

        RTE                                   16.9 m

     Designed TD                                           4534.88 m

    Tolerance at TD                                                    +/- 100 m

   Target Reservoir                                       Lower Fahliyan

   Well Completion              Completed with Cemented 7" liner

Note:

After spud the rig, RTE (16.9) must be adjusted with actual RTE.

                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and

1.3 Reservoir Information

The primary target reservoir layer for the six wells in this Contract is Fahliyan, with a
gross thickness of about 300 m. The Description of Reservoir is as below table.

                            Reservoir (Fahliyan)

                   GOR               1500 SCF/STB

                    H2S Content (ppm)             2000

                  CO2 Content (ppm)              25,800

                        Salinity (ppm as NaCl)           156,000

                  Calcium Content (mg/lit)            5,600

                   Temperature Gradient            1.3°F/100ft

   Note: the relationship between temperature and depth is: Temperature (°F) = 0.013 *
   Depth (ftss) + 85.25

1.4 Program Outline

Well SPH-03 will be spudded as a producer well in the vicinity of the well SPH-01 (about
1000 meters away from the Well SPH-01). This Well will be penetrated finally into the
Lower Khami Group including Gadvan sands and Lower Fahliyan carbonates with
estimated depth of 4535 m (TD).

36" Hole section:

This hole would be drilled from surface to the depth of 104 m, inside the near top of
Aghajari clastic/marly formation with mud weight of 67 to 68 pcf and this hole size is
protected by 30" casing. The relevant casing shoe will be set at the depth of 104 m and
then cemented up.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

 Doc. No:              SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 17 of 222

24" Hole section:

This hole would be penetrated into the Aghajari clastic/Marly formation with mud
weight of 68 to 89 pcf. The hole would also penetrate into the Mishan Marly/Carbonate
formation at the depth of 1402 m, and then will continue to the top of Gachsaran
formation at the depth of 1439.74 m. In addition, during the drilling operation, the top
most of Gachsaran formation will be also drilled to the depth of 1495 m (section TD).
At the end of hole penetration, the required logs including CDR, GR for open hole will
be run, after logging operation, the 18 5/8" casing will be run and cemented at 1495 m,
55m TVD below top of Gachsaran (Member 7) formation.

17" Hole Section:

Drilling this hole would be started from the upper part of Gachsaran formation with
110 to 137 pcf (Max mud weight based on SPH02 and SPH03 : 120pcf) mud weight and
will continue into the entire members of Gachsaran formation (members 1 to 6). The
related mud weight will be increased gradually to 137 pcf at the depth of 1928.5 m. At
the end of hole penetration, the required wire line logs including CDR/GR,4 Arm CALI
Logs will be run, after logging operation, the 13 3/8" x 13-5/8” casing would be run and
set at the depth of 1928.5 m and then cemented up then CCL,GR,CBL, VDL for cased
hole will be run as program.

The mentioned hole size encompasses the big interval of Gachsaran formation,
including of gray marl, anhydrite, red marl, salt, limestone, argillaceous limestone and
claystone.

12 1/4” Hole Section:

This hole would be drilled inside the Asmari formation with 75 to 86 pcf mud weight.
The drilling operation will continue from Lower Asmari to basal part of Sarvak
formation to the depth of 3727 m (TD), Mud weight would be adjusted and increased
to 82 pcf based on hole condition. At the end of this hole drilling, the required logs
including CDR and Full Set logs will be run, after logging operation, the 9 5/8" casing
would be run and set at the depth of 3726 m (Casing Shoe) and then cemented up then
CBL,VDL,CCL,GR,CAST or USIT for cased hole would be run.

The drilled lithology consists of mainly limestone, sandstone, gray marl, argillaceous
limestone, red claystone, anhydrite,  salt and dolostone, belong to the base of

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

 Doc. No:              SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 18 of 222

Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan and Sarvak formations.

8-1/2’’ Hole Section:

This hole would be started from the basal Sarvak formation (about 3726 m) and then
penetrate into the Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan formations respectively to
target depth of 4535 m (TD). At the end of drilling operation, the required logs including
CDR, Full Set Logs will be run. The 7” liner would be run and set at the depth of 4535 m
(Liner Shoe) and then cemented up then CBL,VDL,CCL,GR,CAST or USIT for cased hole
would be run and MTT Log will be run after perform perforation.

The mud weight would be started with 113 pcf and then gradually increases to 135 pcf,
particularly within the Gadvan and Upper Fahliyan pressurized (clay rich) intervals.

The drilled lithology observed during operation consist of mainly limestone, sandstone,
shale, chertified limestone, claystone, argillaceous limestone, belong to the Sarvak,
Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan.

2. Geological Information

2.1 Formation Description

A brief description of the predicted stratigraphical column in the Well SPH-03 has been
taken as follows:

2.1.1 Aghajari Formation (from surface to 1402.12 m)

This huge clastic/marly formation (about 1385 m thickness) is Pliocene-Pleistocene in age and
mostly composed of red claystones/siltstones, interbedded sandstone and red claystone beds.
It is expected that the red claystone intervals increase to the bottom of the formation. The
sandstone and siltstone layers are variably consolidated and generally can be described with
light brown and light gray colors, fine to medium sand grains, well sorted and sometimes are
dominated by sub-angular to sub-rounded quartz grains. The above-mentioned sandstones are
loose (un-consolidated sands), friable to moderately hard form.

The marls are red, reddish brown to pale reddish brown, generally sticky and slightly silty, soft
to firm, grading to claystone. The claystones are red color, sometimes show dark brown,
generally soft, sticky and amorphous, locally some calcareous, grading to gray marl, generally
non-silty and occasionally mottled. 36” and 24” Hole sizes will be penetrated into the Aghajari
formation and then 30” casing shoe will be set at the near top of this formation, approximately
will be located in the depth of 104 m (MD).

2.1.2 Mishan Formation (from 1402.12 to 1439.74 m)

The Mishan formation (about 37.6 m thickness) with Middle Miocene in age,  is mainly
comprised of alternated marls and limestone beds in this well, overlying the topmost
Gachsaran anhydrite layer. 24” Hole size will be penetrated within this small marly/ carbonate
formation.

2.1.3 Gachsaran Formation (from 1439.74 to 1942.41 m)

The Gachsaran (about 502.6 m thickness) formation with Early to Middle Pliocene in
age, is predominantly made up of evaporates (anhydrite) beds, which are interbedded
with gray to red marls. In terms of drilling hazards, the Gachsaran formation can be
subdivided into two (2) main intervals, called Upper and Lower Gachsaran.

The Upper Gachsaran, which corresponds to members 2 to 7,  is predominantly

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 32 of 222

comprised of an alternation between anhydrite and grey marly beds. The marly
succession is gray to light gray color and soft, which is observed as interbedded with
some anhydrite beds. The relevant anhydrite is white, milky, soft, partly crystalline and
medium hard to hard. The red marls are present and show variety of colors, ranged
from red brown to brown, characterized by soft and sticky lithology.  18 5/8” casing
shoe will be set at the near top of Gachsaran formation, in depth of 1495 m (MD).
Otherwise, 17” hole size will be penetrated into the Gachsaran formation.

The Lower Gachsaran unit, usually corresponds to the member 1 or named Cap Rock
unit, including alternated salt and anhydrite beds (in form of hard and crystalline rocks).
It is noteworthy that the basal part of Gachsaran formation is characterized by dark
grey to grey marls, accompanied with probable thin salt beds and interbedded
anhydrite layers. The 13 3/8” casing shoe will be set at the basal part of Gachsaran
formation in the depth of 1928.5 m (MD).

2.1.4 Asmari Formation (from 1942.41 to 2340.48 m)

The Asmari formation (about 398 m thickness) with Oligocene-Miocene age,  is
expected to be subdivided into several distinct lithological units in this well. The top of
Asmari formation is characterized by bioclastic limestone and some dolomitized beds.
Nevertheless, the main body of Upper Asmari unit is mostly comprised of clastic beds
or fine sandstones/ siltstones (corresponds to Ghar Member).

The Upper Asmari sandy layers are interbedded with gray marls, few dolomite and
claystone beds. The main body of sandstones are made up of medium grained sorted,
gray to light gray, medium to coarse sands and partly pyritized. The claystone shows
reddish to light brown color, soft and sticky form. The lower part of Asmari formation
is made up of white, cream, light gray to gray dolomitic limestone/limestone beds. The
12 1/4” hole will be penetrated into this clastic/ carbonate formation.

2.1.5 Pabdeh Formation (from 2340.48 to 2667.23 m)

The Pabdeh formation (Paleocene-Eocene age) with about 326.75 m thickness, is
expected to be subdivided into two (2) stratigraphic units in this Well. The upper part
consists of grey to dark-brown argillaceous limestones, accompanied with some
interbedded marls, representing pelagic facies.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 33 of 222

In terms of microfossil contents, index planktonic microfauna is common throughout
these argillaceous intervals. There are some dark-brown and slightly argillaceous
limestone interlayers. The 12 1/4” hole will be penetrated into this marly/ carbonate
formation.

2.1.6 Gurpi Formation (from 2667.23 to 2919.91 m)

The Gurpi formation (Campanian to Maastrichtian age) with about 252.6 m thickness,
can be subdivided into three (3) stratigraphic units, which are named the Upper Gurpi,
Tarbur Tongue and Lower Gurpi from top to the bottom respectively. The Upper Gurpi
unit is mainly characterized by grey to dark-brownish argillaceous limestone beds,
which are marked by abundant pelagic facies and variable clay contents. Glauconitic
argillaceous limestone to marl is one of the best markers to identify this unit.

The middle part of Gurpi formation corresponds to a shallow marine carbonate stack,
which is named Tarbur Tongue. This particular carbonate interval is developed in the
Sepehr-Jufair  area,  characterized by  bioclastic limestone  lithology and a huge
accumulation of large bioclastic fragments. The Tarbur Tongue is creamy to light gray
limestone stack, indicating moderate to fair reservoir quality (based on nearby wells)
and probable economic hydrocarbon volume.

The Lower Gurpi unit is overlain directly by Tarbur limestone showing some similarities
to the Upper Gurpi unit in terms of lithology and facies types. The Lower Gurpi is mainly
characterized by grey argillaceous limestone layers (planktonic facies also dominant),
which are interbedded by some grey marly intervals. The 12 1/4” hole size will
penetrate this marly/ carbonate formation.

2.1.7 Ilam Formation (from 2919.91 to 3030.41 m)

The Ilam formation with Santonian in age, expected thickness of 110.5 m, known as
one of the main hydrocarbons producing reservoir layers in the Sepehr-Jufair area. The
Ilam formation is made up of grey to brown color limestone intervals, accompanied
with few interbedded marly to claystone beds (rarely pyritic), which will increase
significantly to the top of this formation.

Therefore, the Upper Ilam stack is considered to be an alternation between dolomitic

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 34 of 222

limestone and marly to claystone beds. The above-mentioned dolomitic limestone
interval may have significant reservoir quality and probable hydrocarbon contents.
Although, the main body of Ilam formation, which is named Ilam-Main unit, is the main
reservoir column and essentially comprised of limestone facies. This reservoir unit is
characterized by planktonic facies such as Oligosteginid forms, associated with
negligible clay contents. It is expected to be a big hydrocarbon bearing (more than 30
m) interval in this well.

The Lower Ilam unit, which is informally named Ilam-Poor (in terms of low reservoir
quality), corresponds to a muddy limestone interval, overlying the Laffan unit directly
and usually is marked by negligible hydrocarbon contents (most probably) and fully
saturated by formation water. The 12 1/4” hole will penetrate this marly/ carbonate
formation.

2.1.8 Laffan Unit (from 3030.41 to 3038.41 m)

Stratigraphically, the Laffan unit (about 8 to 10 m thickness) corresponds to the basal
part of Ilam formation indicating Coniacian age and marked by a greenish-grey shale
layers, expected to be seen in this well, overlying the top Sarvak carbonate interval. It
is explained that this key formation was developed in Abadan Plain and offshore
Persian Gulf areas, distinguished by fresh water to coastal Ostracod bearing interval.

This argillaceous formation  significantly exhibits a sealing rock potential in the
Operational Area, capped the Upper Sarvak reservoir. In terms of drilling hazards and
abnormal pressure, this formation does not show any particular problem. The 12 1/4”
will penetrate this argillaceous/ marly formation.

2.1.9 Sarvak Formation (from 3038.41 to 3755.78 m)

The Sarvak formation (about 717.3 m thickness) with Albian/Cenomanian-Turonian in
age is mainly characterized by bioclastic carbonate successions. The Upper Sarvak
interval is the main oil-bearing stack and generally described as a set of light brown to
creamy bioclastic limestone, accompanied with negligible thin marls. On the whole,
Upper Sarvak represents a moderate to fair reservoir quality, which is limited to the
reservoir zones 3 and 4 particularly in the nearby wells. This reservoir unit is correlable
with all nearby wells and significantly marked by Rudist fragments and generally is

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 35 of 222

distinguished by clean Gamma radiation interval.

The middle to lower part of Sarvak formation  is mainly dominated by bioclastic
limestone beds, negligible clay contents, is mostly saturated by formation water.
Ahmadi Shale unit is marked as a continuous argillaceous interval (about 10 to 15 m
thickness), separates the upper and lower parts of Sarvak formation.

The lower to basal part of Sarvak formation (corresponds to the Mauddud unit) is
composed of grain-supported bioclastic limestone beds, which are characterized by
negligible clay contents and some chertified limestone beds. The clay volume shows a
gradual increase in the vicinity of the Sarvak/Kazhdumi boundary.

In terms of drilling hazards and abnormal pressure, there is high risk and high-pressure
interval just can be expected in the basal part of Sarvak formation (near to Top
Kazhdumi boundary), where the clay volume and chertified limestone beds gradually
appeared. It is planned that the hole size 12 1/4” will be mostly penetrated into this
huge carbonate formation. The related 9 5/8” casing shoe will be set at the basal part
of Sarvak formation in the approximate depth of 3726 m (MD) and also the 7” liner
hanger will be set in the same interval (about 3626 m).

2.1.10 Kazhdumi Formation (from 3755.78 to 3953.9 m)

The Kazhdumi formation with Albian in age (198.1 m thickness), starts with (from base
to the top) a thick bedded clastic (sandy) bed (named Azadegan Sand unit), developed
in the nearby wells. The Lower Kazhdumi unit is expected to be a composition of
consolidated sandstone and marly beds (about 45 m thickness). The middle to upper
part of Kazhdumi formation  is predominantly characterized by shallow marine
(Orbitolinid facies) limestone/marly intervals, accompanied with some interbedded
claystones.

The basal clastic unit exhibits fair reservoir quality that expected to be a fully saturated
by formation water. The topmost interval of Kazhdumi formation (about 40 m
thickness) is again dominated by clay/argillaceous muddy facies, marked by planktonic
microfauna and some organic materials. The 8 1/2” hole size will penetrate this marly/
clastic formation.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 36 of 222

2.1.11 Dariyan Formation (from 3953.9 to 4125.45 m)

The Dariyan formation with Aptian in age (171.5 m thickness),  is predominantly
comprised  of  clean  (ignorable  clay  contents)  carbonate  intervals,  which  are
characterized by Orbitolinid facies domination. Stratigraphically, the Dariyan grey to
brown  color  bioclastic limestone  is  situated between two  (2)  clastic bearing/
argillaceous formations including Gadvan (lower) and Kazhdumi (upper) formations
and unlike this carbonate formation does not show any economic hydrocarbon
saturation (based on nearby wells).

It is expected that the clay contents be increased particularly at the basal part of
Dariyan formation, accompanied with some marly beds. In terms of drilling hazards and
abnormal pressure, no particular problem has been reported in this pure carbonate
formation. The 8 1/2” hole will penetrate into this carbonate formation.

2.1.12 Gadvan Formation (from 4125.45 to 4293.88 m)

The Gadvan formation with Barremian to Aptian in age (168.4 m thickness), is expected
to be subdivided into two (2) stratigraphical units in this well, including Lower
(interbedded sandstones and shales) and Upper (dominantly argillaceous/marly) units.
The Gadvan formation can be started with a basal clastic sand/silt package (about 40
m thick.), which is interbedded with some marls/claystone beds and overlying the top
of Fahliyan carbonates. Generally, the Lower Gadvan unit is characterized by alternated
fine grain sandstone and argillaceous interlayers that are developed in the nearby wells
and overlain by Khalij carbonate interval (about 20 m thick.). It is expected to be
encountered with outstanding oil-bearing sand layers, based on what has been
observed in Well SPH-01 and some Jufair wells. The Khalij member usually known as a
muddy limestone interval, characterized by moderate to low reservoir quality and
negligible hydrocarbon volume (based on log interpretation). This interval is expected
to be subdivided into three (3) intervals including two (2) carbonate (Khalij A and Khalij
B) and a claystone package, reported in the nearby wells. The Upper Gadvan unit is
dominantly is characterized by alternated bioclastic limestone (Orbitolinid facies) and
marly/claystone layers, which is comfortably overlain by Dariyan limy formation. In
terms of drilling hazards and abnormal pressure, no particular problem has been
occurred in this clay-rich/clastic bearing formation. Although the formation pressure in
this interval is much more significant than the higher layers, we are faced with a

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                     Production Operations Project
                    Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:             SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03        Page 37 of 222

significant increasing pressure trend. Therefore, the  drilling mud weight should
increase with respect to the recorded pressure. The 8 1/2” hole will penetrate this
marly/clastic formation.

2.1.13  Fahliyan Formation (from 4293.88 to 4535 m)

The Early Cretaceous (Neocomian stage) Fahliyan formation (241 m thickness) is known
as the most important reservoir layer in the Sepehr-Jufair area. In terms of stratigraphy
and subsurface geology, the Fahliyan formation can be subdivided into two (2) units
including Lower and Upper Fahliyan layers.

The Upper Fahliyan (about 100 m thick.)  is characterized by the domination of
argillaceous limestone and interbedded marly intervals, indicating low to non-reservoir
quality.  Stratigraphically, this limestone to marly unit can be correlated with Iraqi
Ratawi formation representing the same age and facies.

The Lower Fahliyan (about 140 m thick.) is expected to be a bioclastic limestone stack
with  noticeable   reservoir   quality  and  economic  hydrocarbon   saturation.
Stratigraphically, the Lower Fahliyan unit can be equivalent with the Iraqi Yamama
formation, developed in the Iraqi/ Arabian areas. The Lower Fahliyan carbonates
correspond to a grey to brown color bioclastic limestone stack in the form of muddy to
grain-supported facies. The 8 1/2” hole will penetrate into this marly/carbonate
formation. 7” liner shoe will be set at the TD (4535 m) and due to increase pressure,
the drilling mud weight should be increased with respect to the well condition.

As previously mentioned, this forecasted stratigraphic column has been provided based
on the reference (SPH-01) and nearby wells (new seismic data is also used). Therefore,
there is still a possibility of slight changes in the depths of the formation tops and the
lithology of some formations.

2.2 Cutting Sample Program

Wellsite geologist is fully responsible to determine the frequency of cutting sampling.
The sampling crew shall take cutting samples based on wellsite geologist’s instruction.
It  is noted that the acquired sand samples from Asmari, Kazhdumi and Gadvan
formations must be preserved unwashed. Note that to identify Rock Type standard
classification  systems  (Dunham  classification  for  carbonates  and  Petti  john
classification for sandstones) must be used.

3. Offset Wells

Wells SPH-01, SPH-SW1, JR-04 and JR-03 were drilled in the Operational Area previously
and they have been considered as reference wells. The geographical coordination of
these wells, in respect to Well SPH-03, are as below:

                                Table 1 Position of nearby wells

The nearest well to the proposed SPH-03, is SPH-01 which was spudded in 2011. This well was
drilled from the surface (Aghajari formation) to the near bottom of the Fahliyan formation
(Lower Fahliyan reservoir layers) and finally was penetrated into the depth of 4577 m (TD).

3.3 Potential Drilling Hazards and mitigation plan

For each section a general list of most probable drilling risk has been summarized in below tables.

                           Potential drilling hazards/Risks for drilling 17” hole

   Potential Hazard                           How to mitigate

                  Gachsaran formation consisting mainly of Anhydrate and Marls with inter beds of salt.
                  Gachsaran formation also consists of marly limestone. This is a potential salt water
                    flow zone.
    Salt water flow    Maintain MW as plan MW.
                  Check Calcium ions while drilling, in case of showing increasing trend, drilling to be
                   stopped.
                  Flow check if fast break experience.
                   Maintain MW as plan MW.
  Loss of circulation   Control running speed to prevent surge effect.
                    Control mud rheology.
                     Closely monitor losses while drilling.
                   Maintain MW as plan MW.
                   Minimize the overbalance (by decreasing mud weight)
   Stuck drill string
                   Minimize stationary time.
                    Increase drill collar and drill string stabilization.
                   Optimize fluid properties in attempts to minimize the risk of sticking.
   Stuck casing due   Avoid creating ledges by easing bit in harder formation and reaming transition
   to hole cleaning,   between formations.
   hole geometry,   Watch casing string weight, understand and monitor casing drag up and down.
  loss/flow or tight   Avoid thick mud cake by controlling fluid loss and solids.
        hole         Circulate hole clean prior to POOH for casing Survey check.
                      Entire bit and/or stabilizers, teeth balling and bottom balling can be reduced by having
                  a sufficiently inhibitive mud.
                      Entire bit Balling risk will be reduced by consistently high flow rates, and good hole
                      cleaning.
                   Ensure of pumping at high flow rates, and sufficient HSI (>2).
                  With tri-cone bits, center jet and oriented nuzzling will also aid preventing balling
                     (oriented nuzzling directs part of the flow onto the cutting structure).
                   Avoid excessive WOB while drilling through Gachsaran formation.
       Bit Balling
                    Actions once balling has been detected:
                   • Bits can be cleared if balling is detected early enough and actions taken.
                   • Reduce WOB when ROP drops, pick up bit off bottom as quickly as possible and
                      increase flow rate.
                   • Spin bit off bottom with high RPM and high flow rate for a period of time
                   • Resume drilling with low weight
                   • Prepare to pump pills (KCL solution pill), leave to soak and try to dissolve/loosen
                       balled material

                             Drilling hazards/risks while drilling in 12-1/4” hole

 Potential Hazard                           How to mitigate
                  Asmari consists of porous limestone and dolomites providing very fast drilling.
                  Asmari is a potential loss zone due to its high porosity.
  Loss circulation    As losses have appeared, drilling with minimum parameter and sweep hole with
                  High filter-loss LCM pill.
                  Prepare to spot conventional LCM plugs.
                 Pabdeh and Laffan Shales could be unstable at varying hole angles, although there
                   are no apparent problems drilling it vertically.
                    Raise mud density immediately if long slender pieces of Pabdeh or Laffan shale
  Shale instability
                    started coming over the shakers.
                  Pabdeh, Laffan and Sarvak formations must be drilled with inhibited mud (mud
                  weight must ensure stability even if severe losses encountered).
                 The mud weight must minimize the risk of differentially stuck pipe when crossing
                   the zones within the reservoir objective.
                  There are several specialized “bridging “products that reduce seepage thereby
                   reducing the likelihood of differential sticking in low permeability zones.
                        Install string stabilizers within the drill string to ensure ‘stand-off from the wellbore.
                  Ensure preventative drilling practices are strictly adhered to  (i.e. moving and
Differential sticking   rotating drill-string as much as possible, minimizing length of unstabilised BHA, use
                    of spiral Drill Collars and HWDP).
                    Control water loss, minimize mud cake building tendency.
                  Monitor filter cake quality.
                 Use of Rotary Steerable Systems will minimize overall contact with the wellbore and
                        will reduce the tendency for differential sticking.
                     Solids removal equipment should be used with full performance and efficiency
                  Maintain Mud weight as plan Mud weight.
                  Monitor mud rheology.
     Tight hole      Ensure sufficient inhibitor exist in mud system.
                   Consider wiper trip if hole condition dictates.
                 To minimize chances of stuck pipe, avoid pulling above 25 Klbs.
      Drill string       Inspect drill string regularly.
     corrosion      Use corrosion inhibitor into mud system.
                   Ledging can be an issue when drilling alternate hard and soft formations particularly
                     at an angle. This can occur when drilling through the reservoir zones. Ledging occurs
                    generally at the top of the harder formations at the interface when whirl or other
                       lateral instability is induced due to the reduced depth of cut. This can be reduced or
                   eliminated by following negative drill break practices and easing the bit into the
 Ledging and hole    harder formation.
      spiraling       Poor weight transfer to the bit can also produce ledging and hole enlargement by
                   reducing the depth of cut at the bit, thus initiating lateral instability and whirl.
                 The higher the bend angle the more likely hole spiraling will occur. To overcome this
                 problem a long gauge bit or turbo-back stabilizer can be used. Another method of
                   reducing hole spiraling is to reduce the distance from bit face to bent housing.
                 Review the offset wells for problems encountered.

                  Hole angles between 45° and 65° present the most problems with drilled cuttings
                   having a tendency to slide down the annulus and ‘pack off’. Hole angles greater than
                   65° do not present additional problems in this regard since the drilled cuttings are
Poor hole cleaning
                  supported by sliding friction against the wellbore and become more stable as
                     discrete cuttings beds. Use good hole cleaning practices to prevent formulation of
                     cutting beds (Rotation, High GPM, Thin Sweeps. . .)
                       Utilizing DV collar.

               The mud weight must ensure stability within the Kazhdumi and Gadvan Shale and
                minimize the risk of differentially sticking when crossing the zones within the reservoir
                  objective (Fahliyan).
                     Installing string stabilizers within the drill string to ensure stand-off from the wellbore
                    in permeable reservoir formations will compromise directional (build) capability.
               Use of Rotary Steerable Systems will minimize overall contact with the wellbore and
   Stuck Pipe
                      will reduce the tendency for differential sticking, particularly in longer extended reach
                    wells.
                Ensure preventative drilling practices are strictly adhered to i.e. moving and rotating
                      drill-string as much as possible, minimizing length of unestablished BHA, use of spiral
                       Drill Collars and HWDP. These measures however may not be practical in wells where
                 the build to horizontal is a critical objective.
              Most of well control problems occur while tripping out due to swabbing (tripping
  Well Control    speed) or failure to fill the hole with proper fluid volume; trip sheet has to be filled

3.3.1 Stuck Pipe Mechanism

To identify the stuck pipe mechanism, the below table can be used as per below instruction:

    1.  Select the situations that you see and circle all the figures in the same row
    2. Add all figures for each column
    3. The highest score indicates the stuck pipe mechanism

  Pipe motion prior to
                          Pack-off/Bridge off     Differential Sticking    Wellbore Geometry
        sticking?

      Move up                2                   0                   2
      Rotating up               0                   0                   2

     Rotating down             0                   0                   2
          Static                 2                   2                   0

   Pipe motion after
        sticking?

     Down free               0                   0                   2
   Down restricted             1                   0                   2
   Down impossible            0                   0                   0

        sticking?

Loss Circulation Mitigation:

24” hole section loss control:

Downhole Losses

   Concentration of LCM will vary according to the size of losses. In the event of seepage losses, no specific
   action will be taken but close monitoring of the loss rate over the time and drilled depth shall be made.

   In the event of moderate to high rate of well bore loss, prepare pills at different grades and concentrations,
  and spot it over thief zone. The concentration may vary between 20 to 100 ppb.

  Below procedure is supposed to be followed in case that various rates of losses are encountered in the
   course of drilling, though the most effective plan is the one which is customized based on actual situation
  on site:
Losses less than 10 bbl/hr:

   ➢  No action to be taken, but close monitoring of the loss shall be made
Losses 10 - 30 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load Kwik-Seal F in system up to 10 ppb

         If no success, then:
   ➢  Pump 50 bbl LCM pill containing:
           ▪  3-6 ppb Mica Medium
           ▪  7-12 ppb Walnut Shells Fine
           ▪  7-12 ppb Kwik-Seal Fine & Medium
           ▪  3-6 ppb CaCO3-M

     Note: System is loaded gradually from low concentration to maximum mentioned ranges.
Losses 30 – 100 bbl/hr:

  ➢  Load CaCO3 Medium & Coarse in active system up to 5 ppb of each
  ➢  Load Kwik-Seal F in system up to 10 ppb
     If needed / required; shaker-man should be ready for managing a part of the return
       flow toward the mud pits by-pass the shakers to avoid screens get blinded and LCM
       get wasted. However, this is a matter of operation-dictated decision.

     If no success, then:
  ➢  Stop Drilling
  ➢  Pull out string above the loss zone
  ➢  Spot 60-90 bbl pill containing:
       ▪  Pre-Hydrated Bentonite 5-10 ppb
       ▪  6-10 lb/bbl Kwik-Seal F

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 52 of 222

       ▪  6-10 lb/bbl Kwik-Seal M
       ▪  6-10 lb/bbl Walnut-Shells F
       ▪  6-10 lb/bbl Mica M
       ▪  20-30 lb/bbl CaCO3-F, M
       ▪  Repeat this pill for two times
  ➢   If LCM pill at above concentration cannot pass through the string (bit nuzzles, etc.), we need
        to pump the pill through a Mule-Shoe while following the procedure of the next state.
Losses more than 100 bbl/hr to Total Losses:

   In the event of severe losses, Squeeze Pills, Barite or CaCO3 or Cement Plugs, Silicate Pill or
   Cross-Linking Pills should be pumped to minimize losses.

17” Hole section loss control:

Downhole Losses

  The concentration of LCM will vary according to the size of losses. In the event of seepage losses,
   treat system with a finer grade of LCM at 10-15 lb/bbl.

   In the event of moderate to high rate of well bore loss, prepare  pills at different graded
   concentrations and spot over thief zone. The concentration may vary between 25-95 lb/bbl. In
   order to avoid LCM plugging MWD tools a circulation sub should be installed in the drill string. The
 MWD engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be pumped through
 MWD Tools. Ensure that the drilling displace the LCM pill out of the string prior to shutting down
   the mud pumps.

   In the event of severe losses, cross linking pills should be pumped to minimize losses.

  The following plan is to be followed prior to intersecting the loss zone while drilling in case losses
   are encountered:

The following plan is to be followed in case losses are encountered:

Losses less than 10 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5ppb.

   ➢  Load synthetic fibre in system up to 0.1 ppb
   ➢  Load KWIK SEAL-F in system up to 10 ppb
Losses 10 - 30 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5ppb.
   ➢  Load Synthetic Fibre in system up to 0.1ppb

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 53 of 222

   ➢  Pump 50bbl LCM pill contains:
           ▪  5 ppb Mica-M.
           ▪  10 ppb Walnut Shells Fine.
           ▪  10 ppb KWIK-SEAL-F.
           ▪  5 ppb CaCO3-M

Note: According to condition, the system will be load from low concentration to maximum mentioned

range

Losses 30 – 100 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5 ppb.
   ➢  Load Synthetic Fibre in system up to 0.1 ppb.
   ➢  Load KWIK SEAL-Fine in system up to 10 ppb.
                       If not success then:
   ➢   Stop Drilling.
   ➢   Pull out string above the loss zone
   ➢   Spot 100-150 bbl pill contains (through PBL Sub);
              ▪  10 lb/bbl KWIK-SEAL-F
              ▪  15 lb/bbl KWIK-SEAL-M
              ▪  15 lb/bbl Walnut-F.
              ▪  15 lb/bbl Mica-M.
              ▪  30 lb/bbl CaCO3-F & M
              ▪  Pre hydrated Bentonite 5-10 ppb

Repeat the previous pill minimum of two times.

Losses more than 100 bbl/hr:

    ➢  Silicate pill
    ➢ Squeeze 50 bbl of Bentonite-Diesel Oil-Cement squeeze. The pumping ratio would be
           1:1 mud to gunk.
    ➢ Pump Cement squeeze.

If Total Losses and Well Flows:

Pump Cement squeeze.

12 1/4” Hole section loss control:

Downhole Losses

   Concentration of LCM will vary according to the size of losses. In the event of seepage losses, no
   specific action will be taken but close monitoring of the loss rate over the time and drilled depth

                        Production Operations Project

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 54 of 222

   shall be made. In the event of moderate to high rate of downhole loss, prepare pills at different
  graded concentrations and spot over thief zone. The concentration may vary between 25-35
   lb/bbl. Ensure that LCM pill is displaced out of the string prior to shutting down the mud pumps.

  The following plan is to be followed prior to intersecting the loss zone while drilling notorious
   formations and in case losses are encountered:

The following plan is to be followed in case losses are encountered:

Losses less than 10 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5ppb.

   ➢  Load Synthetic Fibre in system up to 0.1 ppb
   ➢  Load KWIK SEAL-F in system up to 10 ppb
Losses 10 - 30 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5ppb.
   ➢  Load Synthetic Fibre in system up to 0.1ppb
   ➢  Pump 50bbl LCM pill contains:
           ▪  5 ppb Mica-M.
           ▪  10 ppb Walnut Shells Fine.
           ▪  10 ppb KWIK-SEAL-F.
           ▪  5 ppb CaCO3-M

Note: According to condition, the system will be load from low concentration to maximum mentioned

range

Losses 30 – 100 bbl/hr:

   ➢  Load CaCO3-M in active system up to 5 ppb.
   ➢  Load Synthetic Fibre in system up to 0.1 ppb.
   ➢  Load KWIK SEAL-Fine in system up to 10 ppb.
                       If not success then:
   ➢   Stop Drilling.
   ➢   Pull out string above the loss zone
   ➢   Spot 100-150 bbl pill contains (through PBL Sub);
              ▪  10 lb/bbl KWIK-SEAL-F
              ▪  15 lb/bbl KWIK-SEAL-M
              ▪  15 lb/bbl Walnut-F.
              ▪  15 lb/bbl Mica-M.
              ▪  30 lb/bbl CaCO3-F & M
              ▪  Pre hydrated Bentonite 5-10 ppb

Repeat the previous pill minimum of two times.

Losses more than 100 bbl/hr:

    ➢  Silicate pill
    ➢ Squeeze 50 bbl of Bentonite-Diesel Oil-Cement squeeze. The pumping ratio would be
           1:1 mud to gunk.
    ➢ Pump Cement squeeze.
If Total Losses and Well Flows:

     Pump Cement squeeze.

8.5” Hole section loss control:

Downhole Losses

   Losses/Differential sticking  will be minimized/avoided by the planned use of properly sized
  bridging/weight material (and Polymer)  in conjunction with appropriate  drilling practices.  If
  seepage losses are noted on the well no specific action will be taken but close monitoring of the
   loss rate is to be made. For moderate loss rates addition of CaCO3 50 micron and 150mic. is
  recommended to ensure that there is adequate correctly sized material to act as bridging agents.

  CaCO3 will minimize filtrate/whole mud invasion of the reservoir and reduce potential problems of
  losses and differential sticking. The fluid loss and cake quality are two of the most important mud
   properties to prevent or alleviate potential stuck pipe. Note that, the maintenance of the correct
  amount and size of bridging material cannot be over-stressed.

Seepage losses Less than 10 bbl/hr.

  Load the mud system with 5 ppb of CaCO3 M LCM. Some of the CaCO3-300 will be separated
  on the shale shaker. Add 4 sacks per 5 meters drilled to compensate the separated amount on
  the shakers.

Losses from 10-30 bbl/hr.

  ➢ Load CaCO3 M in system up to 5ppb.
  ➢ Pump 45-60bbl of the following pill:
           ▪  5ppb CaCO3 75mic
           ▪  10-15 ppb CaCO3 200-300mic or Oyster shell F
           ▪  10-15 ppb CaCO3 600mic or Oyster shell M
Losses from 30-50 bbl/hr.

  ➢ Load CaCO3 M in system up to 5ppb.
  ➢ Pump 45-60bbl of the following pill:
           ▪  10-15ppb CaCO3 75mic
           ▪  20-30 ppb CaCO3 200-300mic or Oyster shell F
           ▪  20-30 ppb CaCO3 600mic or Oyster shell M

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 56 of 222

Losses from 50-100 bbl/hr.

  ➢ Load CaCO3 M in system up to 5ppb.
  ➢ Pump 45-60bbl of the following pill:
           ▪  10-15ppb CaCO3 75mic
           ▪  20-30 ppb CaCO3 200-300mic or Oyster shell F
           ▪  20-30 ppb CaCO3 600mic or Oyster shell M
           ▪  10-20ppb CaCO3 1000mic
           ▪  10-20ppb CaCO3 2000mic
Losses from more than 100 bbl/hr.

Calcium Carbonate plug shall be pumped

4. Sequence of Operation

4.1 36” Hole Section

4.1.1 General

  Casing Point                  30” Casing @ 104 m

  Formation                      Aghajari Formation

  Risks & Hole problems           Rig Subbase Wash out / Erosion, Mud Loss, Almost Problem Free

  Deviation Control           TOTCO

  Logging                    N/A

                                                 Spud Mud

                       MW (pcf)                       67-68

                                                       Vis. (sec/qt)                     >90

                                      PV (cp)                                        -
  Drilling Fluid Parameters
                                       YP (lb./100ft²)                                   -

                               10 Sec / 10 Min Gel (lb./100ft²)                        -

                                         API FL (ml/ 30min)                NC

                                      PH                           9.5 - 10.5

                                GPM                       850-1000

  Drilling Parameters                WOB (KLBs)                      20-45

                                 RPM                        80-120

4.1.2 Sequence of Operation

1.  Check Pre-Spud Check List. Make up 36” BHA (24'' Bit & 36'' Hole Opener as tandem) as per BHA
    design. Tag formation at ±10.5m.

Note:
   ▪  Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in serviceable condition.
   ▪  Ensure that adequate fishing tools for this section of hole are available.
   ▪  Enough Mud Material, LCM and Cement Should be Available before spud the rig to use in

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 60 of 222

       case of needing.
   ▪  1 Set cement pump truck should be available on well site before start drilling.
   ▪  Mud Logging unit should be available from rig spud.

2.  Drill ahead formation down to 104 m (30” casing point in Aghajari formation) using hi-vis
   bentonite pill. Pay enough attention to drill straight hole. Control flow rate and take care of wash
   outs below rig sub base.

Note:
   ▪  TD should be adjusted to have 2 m of rat hole below casing shoe.
   ▪  The exact TD will be determined based on casing tally.
   ▪  Run Survey to Check inclination @ 50 & 100 mt (to ensure the deviation is below 1 degree)

3.  At TD, Circulate and clean the hole. Observe the well. Perform Wiper Trip Prior to running casing.
   Spot viscous mud @ Well Bottom. POOH to surface. Check Bit.

4.  Perform Pre-Job Safety meeting (PJSM).

5. R/Up CSG/ Running Equipment.

6. Run 30" Casing 234# to 104 Mt with stinger shoe to bottom as per casing program. Casing shoe
   should be adjusted to have 2 m of rat hole and enough stick up (1.5 m on rig floor). Fill casing by
   non-LCM fresh mud.

  ➢ Do not exert much load and compressing stress on the shoe if encountered tight hole and
       ledges.
  ➢ Make sure that 2 No. of 2” nipple had been welded on conductor and it is plugged w/ a quick
      union low pressure plug.

7. Run stinger on 5" DP’s using the “C” Plate and 5” x 30” centralizer. Sting in. M/U & test cementing
    lines. Circulate for 1.2 or till shaker clean volume (Maximum allowable pressure: 150 psi). While
    circulating, check the DPs annulus for any return. Use all DPs weight on the string if observing
   any minor leakage through DPs annulus.

8. Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be
   done in due time. Displace with mud (1-2 bbl. Short Displace). Monitor the pressures. Record
   and report the final pressure, Sting out. Check the float. POOH one stand, Circulate to flush/clean

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 61 of 222

     drill pipe. POOH to surface. L/D Stinger While W.O.C to Get Hard, perform top Job If Needed.

Note:
   ▪  Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top-up jobs
      While W.O.C to Get Hard.

9.  After cement returns to cellar floor, open the 2" nipple on conductor & flush it.

10. If there is no cement return to surface, a top-up job should be performed to fill the annulus. Use
   same slurry weight as the primary cement slurry. The cement should be accelerated with max.
   (2%) Calcium Chloride BWOC and no other additives. Wait for the top job cement to get hard.

11. Slack Off Weight. Check Cement by slack off weight and check for any movement.

12. Rough Cut Conductor Pipe and 30" Casing, Dress & Bevel Casing Neck. Prepare and Install 29-
    1/2'' Flange (Locking flange). Check Cement by slack off weight. Check for any movement.

13. N/U BOP (29-1/2" Diverter- 500 psi) Bell Nipple & flow line and Related Lines & Valves.

14. Perform diverter function Test.

15. P/U Next BHA and Stand Back 5" DP's as needed.

16. RIH W/ New BHA and 24" Bit #1 (1-3-5) to Tag Shoe, Drill Out Shoe & cement below shoe.

17. Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:

    •   Bit Reports / Record
    •   Casing Tally
    •   Casing Running Report
    •  Cement Report
    •   Hole Section Summary
    •  Wellhead diagram with dimensions and serial numbers
    •  BOP’s Schematics with all related dimensions
    •  BOP’s test Report
    •  CDAS data & Graph

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 62 of 222

4.2 24” Hole Section

4.2.1 General

   Casing Point                  18-5/8” Casing @ 1495 m

   Formation                       Aghajari, Mishan and Gachsaran Formation

   Risks & Hole problems          Tight Hole, Stuck, Bit Balling, Loss Circulation

   Deviation Control           CDR

   Logging                  CDR + GR

                         PHB then Salt/ KCl /PHPA/ Polymer

                       MW (pcf)                     68 - 89

                                                       Vis. (sec/qt)                      40-50

                                      PV (cp)                      ALAP
   Drilling Fluid Parameters
                                       YP (lb./100ft²)                     15-25

                               10 Sec / 10 Min Gel (lb./100ft²)              6-18

                                         API FL (ml/ 30min)                   <5

                                      PH                        9 - 10

                                GPM                       850-1000

   Drilling Parameters               WOB (KLBs)                      20-45

                                 RPM                        80-120

4.2.2 Sequence of Operation

1.  After D/O of 30'' Casing shoe and Cement Below Shoe, drilling 24" Hole to 107 Mt with 24" Bit
   #1 would be done.

2.  Circulate & Clean the Hole. Run TOTCO Survey. Adjust bentonite mud weight and circulate to
   have unified mud in hole. Perform FIT to EMW of 89 pcf.

3. POOH, P/U New Bit & Drilling BHA, (24" Bit #2), RIH to Bottom.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 63 of 222

Note:
   ▪ For the first run, only one stabilizer will be used and based on TOTCO result, at the bit life trip,
       the using of second stabilizer will be decided.

4.  Drilling 24" Hole to 555 m or Bit Life.

5.  CBU, Run TOTCO Survey. POOH, Change bit, RIH W/24'' Bit #3.

6.  Cont. Drilling 24" Hole to 1005 m.

7.  CBU, Run TOTCO Survey. POOH, Change bit (to PDC Bit), RIH W/24'' Bit #4.

8.  Circulate and Clean the Hole. Adjust new mud weight (Salt/KCl/PHPA/Polymer) and displace the
   hole with new mud.

Note:
   ▪ Due to limitation of surface volume, maybe it needed to displace to new mud at 400 m Depth.
        It will be decided at due time based on the situation

9.  Continue Drilling 24" Hole to 1495 m. (section TD) CBU, Run TOTCO Survey.

10. Perform Condition Trip Prior to Run Casing. POOH and L/D Bit

11. Run Well Survey log and GR Log at TD.

12. Perform PJSM.

13. Rig Up Casing Running Equipment. Run 18 5/8" Casing to 1495 m.

14. Perform cement job for 18-5/8” Casing using cement plug.

15. W.O.C to Get Hard, perform top Job If Needed.

16. Slack off Weight, Check for pipe movement. Rough Cut 30" Casing & Lay Down Landing Joint,
   Remove Landing Flange.

17. Rough Cut 18 5/8" casing, Dress & Bevel Casing Neck, Install C.H.H 18 5/8'' Slip lock on casing *
   20-3/4" 3K & DSA Locking Tie down Flange. Test the same.

18. N/U 20-3/4" 3K BOP's Stack. Meanwhile Perform Top job if Required, BOP Test as NIOC Policy.

19. P/U Next BHA as per plan and Stand DP's as needed.

   RIH W/ New BHA and 17" R. Bit #1. Drill out Shoe track & CMT below shoe. Monitor closely and
   Report Drill out Parameters.

Note:

In case of no bumping during cement job, CIT should be done before drilling shoe track.

                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

4.3 17” Hole Section

4.3.1  General

    Casing Point                13 5/8” Casing @ 1928.5 m

   Formation                   Gachsaran

                                     Tight Hole, Stuck Pipe, Bit Balling, Swabbing and Well Flow, Mud
    Risks & Hole Problems
                                  Losses

    Deviation control            CDR/ GR

    Logging                    4 Arm Caliper/GR/CDR + CBL-VDL/CCL/GR

                                                              Salt Sat. / Polymer

                        MW (pcf)                     110-137

                                                         Vis. (sec/qt)                   40 - 60

                                        PV (cp)                     ALAP

    Drilling Fluid Parameters                YP (lb./100ft²)                    18-30

                                 10 Sec / 10 Min Gel (lb./100ft²)            >7/<20

                                           API FL (ml/ 30min)                  <3

                                       PH                        10

                                         KCL (lb./bbl.)                                   -

                                 GPM                       800-900

    Drilling Parameters                  WOB                      20-50 KLBs

                                  RPM                       120-180

4.3.2 Sequence of Operation

1.  After D/O of 18 5/8'' Casing shoe and Cement below Shoe, Resume Drilling Formation to 1500
  m with 17" Bit #1.

2.  Circulate & Clean the Hole. Adjust mud weight and circulate to have unified mud in hole. Perform
    FIT to EMW of 137 pcf.

3.  Circulate, Clean the Hole, Condition Mud & work on Junk Sub, POOH to Surf.

4. P/U New Bit & Drilling BHA (17" PDC BIT #2 & New BHA), RIH to Bottom.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 65 of 222

5.  Drill 17" Hole to 1928.5 m (section TD), (While Increase MW. Stepwise as Program & based on hole
    condition).

6.  Run Survey Each 300 m Drilled.

7.  Perform Condition TRIP Prior to Logging, C.B.U @ BTM, POOH and L/D Bit.

8.  Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY.

9.  Run CDR/GR, 4 Arm CALI Logs & CBL/VDL/CCL/GR Log inside 18 5/8" Casing in three runs.

10. Perform Condition TRIP Prior to Run Casing. perform wiper trip to shoe and observe well @ shoe. Spot
   100 bbl. hi-vis pill @ bottom.

11. Perform PJSM.

12. Rig Up Casing Running Equipment. Run 13-3/8" & 13-5/8” Casing to 1928.5 m.

13. Circulate 1.2 casing volume & perform Cement job as Per Program.

14. Set the 13-3/8" Casing hanger, Slack off Weight, Check for pipe movement. N/D BOP's, Rough Cut and
   L/D Landing Joint. Dress & bevel casing neck.

15. Install C.H.S. 20-3/4" 3K BTM * 13-5/8" 5K TOP, & Adaptor Flange 13 5/8'' 5K BTM - 10K TOP. Test P-Seal
   and cavity w/max 80% collapse pressure.

16. N/U 13-5/8" 10K BOP's Stack.

17. Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY.

18. P/U and M/U Next BHA and Stand 5" DP's as Needed.

19. RIH W/ New BHA and 12-1/4” R. Bit #1 (1-3-5), Drill out Shoe track. Displace the Hole with 75 PCF
    KCl/Glycol/Polymer/ CaCo3 Mud before drill out shoe. Drill out 13 3/8'' Casing Shoe & CMT below shoe.

Note:

In case of no bumping during cement job, CIT should be done before drilling shoe track.

Note:

“The well should be closely monitored during all phases of the cementing operation. The BOPs
should not be nipple down until you are sure that the well will not flow.”

 4.4.2 Sequence of Operation

1.  After D/O of 13 3/8'' Casing shoe and Cement below Shoe, Cont. Drilling 12-1/4" Hole to 1931.5
  m (W/ 12-1/4” Bit #1 1-3-5).

5.  Cont. Drilling Down 12-1/4" Hole to 2330 m.

7.  Take single shot every 250m drilled.

8.  Cont. Drilling Down 12-1/4" Hole to 2670 m. take single shot every 250m.

9.  Perform Wiper TRIP, Observe the well. Increase MW based on hole Condition gradually.

10. POOH To Surf, L/D Bit.

11. Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY.

12. RIH W/ Drilling BHA To Btm. Continue Drilling Down 12-1/4” Hole to 3020 m, Perform Wiper
    TRIP. Increase MW based on hole Condition gradually.

13. Cont. Drilling Down 12-1/4” Hole to 3370 m, Perform Wiper TRIP. Increase MW based on hole
   Condition gradually.

14. Based on the Bit / BHA Performance it may be decided to POOH. Whether else Cont. Drilling
   Down 12-1/4” Hole to 3726 m (TD). Take single shot every 250m.

15. Perform Condition TRIP Prior to Logging, C.B.U @ BTM, POOH and L/D Bit.

16. R/U logging Equipment and Perform PJSM since the hole is exposed to Asmari, Ilam, Sarvak
   Formations.

17. Run Full Set & CDR Logs.

18. Perform Condition TRIP prior to run Casing.

19. R/U Casing Running Equipment, Perform PJSM.

20. Run 9-5/8" Casing to 3726 m, as program.

21. Perform Cement job as Per Plan.

22. Set the 9-5/8" CSG hanger, N/D BOP's, Rough Cut and L/D Landing Joint. Dress & beveled casing
   neck.

23. Install T.H.S 13 5/8" -5K * 11"-10K & Adaptor Flange 11'' 10K BTM - 13 5/8'' 10 K TOP. Test P-Seal
   and cavity with max 80% casing collapse pressure.

24. N/U 13 5/8"-10 K BOP's Stack Meanwhile Perform Top job if required.

25. Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY.

26. P/U & M/U Next 8.5'' BHA to Drill out Shoe track, Shoe, CMT below Shoe.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 68 of 222

27. RIH W/ New BHA and 8-1/2" Bit#1 (1-3-5) (Junk Sub Included) and Drill Out Shoe Track. Disp.
  Mud in Hole with 113 pcf Mud prior to drill out shoe & CMT below shoe. Drill out Shoe & CMT
   below shoe.

Note:

In case of no bumping during cement job, CIT should be done before drilling shoe track.

NOTE:
  ➢ “The well should be closely monitored during all phases of the cementing operation. The BOPs
       should not be nipple down until you are sure that the well will not flow.”

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project

4.5 8-1/2” Hole Section

4.5.1 General

   Casing/Liner Point                 7’’ Liner Shoe @ 4535, L. Lap @ 3626 m

   Formation                   Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan, Fahliyan

   Risks & Hole Problem          Stuck Pipe, Tight Holes, Well Flow, Mud Losses,

   Deviation Control           CDR

                                 CDR/Full Set Logs
   Logging
                            CAST or USIT/CBL-VDL/CCL/GR/MTT

                                               SALT / KCL / POLYMER

                                                                        113-135                       MW (pcf)

                                                                    45 - 55                                                       Vis. (sec/qt)

                                                                   ALAP                                      PV (cp)

                                                                           18-28   Drilling Fluid Parameters               YP (lb./100ft²)

                                                                        >8/<20                               10 Sec / 10 Min Gel (lb./100ft²)

                                                                                 9.5 - 10.5                                     PH

                                        KCL (lb./bbl.)                    15 - 21

                                  HPHT Fluid Loss                    <5

                               GPM                        400-600

   Drilling Parameters                 WOB                         10-35

                                 RPM                         80-120

4.5.2 Sequence of Operation

1.  After D/O of 9 5/8'' Casing shoe and Cement below Shoe, Cont. Drilling 8-1/2" Hole to 3730 m (8-1/2"
    Bit #1).

2. Work on Junk Sub, Circulate, Clean the Hole. Perform FIT to EMW of 115 pcf.

3. POOH To Surf. Observe the well.

4.  P/U 9-5/8" Suitable Scrapper, RIH to Casing Shoe, Perform Scrapping.

 5.  CIRC Btm`s Up, POOH to Surf, L/D Scrapper.

 6. R/Up Logging Tools, Run USIT/CAST Log inside 9 5/8" Casing, L/D Logging Tools.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

  Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 70 of 222

7. P/UP New 8-1/2" PDC Bit #2, RIH with New BHA to Bottom.

8.  Drill 8-1/2" Hole Down to 4030 m by 8-1/2" PDC Bit#2.

9.  C.B.U, Clean the Hole. Perform Wiper TRIP.

10. Drilling 8-1/2" Hole Down to 4330 m by 8-1/2" PDC Bit#2.

11. C.B.U, Clean the Hole. Perform Wiper TRIP.

12. Drilling 8-1/2" Hole Down to TD @ 4535 m by 8-1/2" PDC Bit#2.

13. C.B.U., Clean the Hole, Perform Hole Condition trip prior to logging. POOH and L/D Bit. R/U
    logging Tools. Perform PJSM.

NOTE:

 ➢ Due to performing several logging operations in this section, 2-7/8" * 7" variable rams must
     be available. Therefore, no need to change rams prior to logging.

14. Run Full Set and CDR Logs as per program.

15. Perform Condition TRIP, Prior To Run 7" Liner.

16. R/U liner running tools. Perform PJSM.

17. Run & Hang & perform cement job 7" Liner.

18. POOH, L/D setting tools.

19. P/U 8-1/2" R. Bit (Including Scrapper), RIH to Liner Lap.

20. CIRC Btm`s Up, POOH to Surf, L/D 8-1/2" Bit.

21. RIH with 5-7/8” bit drill out Pack off bushing to LDC

22. CIRC Btm`s UP. POOH To Surf.

23. Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY when at Surf.

24. P/U Dry Test Equip & run to 50 m above L-lap. Perform Dry test.

  DRY TEST PROCEDURE:
 ➢ Remove wear bushing
 ➢ RIH W/DST tools (Fill up string with water cushion each 5 stands & observe cushion level to make
     2000 psi as Driving Force. Max. RIH speed must be 8 stands per hour)
 ➢ After reaching packer depth (±50 m above liner lap) make up head & surface equipment &
     pressure test (packer should not set on casing connection), Set packer
 ➢ Open the tools for 3 hours or reach water cushion to surface.
 ➢ Close the tools for 6 hours & drop shearing bar & reverse circulation @ the sixth hour, Unset
      packer, Circulate bottom up (CBU)

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                        Production Operations Project
                       Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

   Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03          Page 71 of 222

➢ Observe the well, POOH

 25. Secure the Well, POOH to Surf, L/D Test Equipment.

 26. P/U 7" Suitable Scrapper, RIH to liner Shoe, Scrap. CIRC Btm`s Up, POOH to Surf, L/D Scrapper.

 27. R/Up Logging Tools, Run USIT/CAST Log inside 7" liner, L/D Logging Tools.

 28. P/U 7" Suitable Scrapper, RIH to TD, Scrap. CIRC Btm`s Up, Displace 30 bbl suitable perforation
     fluid (CaCl2).

 29. Test BOP Stack and all Related Lines as NIOC POLICY.

 30. R/U TCP equipment, RIH TCP (for Shoot and Pull), perform TCP correlation, Perforate selected
     intervals of Fahliyan formation, POOH.

 31. RIH MTT/MFC to confirm perforation interval, POOH.

 32. RIH w/ 5 7/8'' Bit To top of perforation interval, Displace Hole with completion fluid (CaBr2).
   POOH and L/d DPS.

 33. R/U and prepare all equipment for Running Completion String, Running Completion String as
    program.

 34. N/D BOP, Install X-Mass tree and test

 35. Open SSD, reverse circulation and displace annulus and tubing fluid (CaBr2) with packer fluid.
    Close SSD and pressure test, Retrieve plug and prong.

 36. Rig up and pressure test surface line and equipment.

 37. Flow the well by Coiled Tubing, perform acid wash, POOH CT and flow the well to cleanup and
    perform PLT

 38. Close choke manifold.

 39. Inflow Test TRSCSSSV (Well pressure below TRSCSSSV & 500 psi above TRSCSSSV), If injectivity
      is good, Bullhead solvent & diesel to the well & if injectivity is not good, displace the well with
     diesel by CT.

 40. Rig up slick-line & perform pressure test, set plug & prong in No-Go landing nipple, perform
    plug & completion string test (4500/7500psi for 15min), Retrieve protection sleeve.

 41. Rig up BPV running/retrieval equipment, Pressure test the same & install one-way check valve.

 42. Close 3SV and X-Mas tree valves except BMV

 43. Rig Released.

    6.1.4 Critical Speed Analysis BHA#2 – 24”

            BHA Dynamics – Vibration Analysis Parameters

 WOB                    40 kip          Starting Speed                      60 rpm
 Torque at Bit             1,500 ft-lbf       Ending Speed                      170 rpm
 Bit RPM                 90 rpm          Excitation Frequency Factor        3 (for Rock Bit)

        6.1.4.1 Stress Components

Note:
•  The optimum range for RPM, based on the stress components plot, is 85 to 95, 110 to 120 and
   130 to 140.
•  At RPM 85, 95 to 105 and 125 the string stress concentration is high, specially shear and bending
    stress.
•  The maximum axial stress is likely to occur at HWDP according to the position plot at 90 rpm,
   the position plot displays the position of the relative stress vs. distance from the bit at specific
   RPM.
•  Based on Displacement plot, the abnormal displacement happens at 95 to 105 rpm.
•   Final optimum RPM and other drilling parameters shall be revised during the operation based
   on drilling condition.

        6.2.4.1 Stress Components

   increased respectively).
•  At RPM 95, 105 to 115, 130 and 170 the string stress concentration is high, specially shear and
   bending stress.
•  The maximum axial stress is likely to occurred at HWDP and DC according to the position plot at
   90 rpm, the position plot displays the position of the relative stress vs. distance from the bit at
    specific RPM.

    at 170 rpm.

    6.3.4 Critical Speed Analysis BHA#4 – 17”

            BHA Dynamics – Vibration Analysis Parameters

 WOB                 40 to 50 kip        Starting Speed                      60 rpm
 Torque at Bit             1,500 ft-lbf       Ending Speed                      170 rpm
 Bit RPM                 120 rpm         Excitation Frequency Factor          6 (for PDC)

        6.3.4.1 Stress Components

Note:
•  The optimum range for RPM, based on the stress components plot, is 110 to 150.
•  At RPM 100 and 160 the string stress concentration is high, specially shear and bending stress.
•  The maximum axial stress is likely to occurred at HWDP and DC according to the position plot at
   120 rpm, the position plot displays the position of the relative stress vs. distance from the bit at
    specific RPM.
•  Based on Displacement plot, the abnormal displacement happens at 95 to 105 and 160 to 170
   rpm.
•   Final optimum RPM and other drilling parameters shall be revised during the operation based
   on drilling condition.

10.2 Casing/Liner Design Criteria

104 m

1495 m

1928.5 m

                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 170 of 222

10.2.1 Casing 30"

Suggested depth for surface 30” casing shoe is about 104 m (Aghajari formation) in
order to seal off the shallow strata, and will be cemented up to the surface.

10.2.2 Casing 18 5/8"

Aghajari Clastic / Marly and Mishan Marly formations will be covered by this casing. The
18 5/8” casing shoe will be set in the most upper part of Gachsaran formation at the
approximate depth of 1495 m MD.

10.2.3 Casing 13 3/8" & 13 5/8”

The penetrated hole in the Gachsaran formation will be covered by tapered 13 3/8" and
13 5/8” casing and the relevant casing shoe will be set inside Cap rock unit of Gachsaran
formation right above top of Asmari formation. The approximate depth of this casing
shoe is about 1928.5 m MD.

10.2.4 Casing 9 5/8"

The penetrated hole in the Asmari, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan and Sarvak formations will be
covered by this casing. The 9 5/8” casing shoe will be set at the basal Sarvak or near top
of Kazhdumi formations in approximate depth of 3726 m MD.

10.2.5 Liner 7"

The penetrated hole in the Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan formations will be
covered by 7" liner and the liner shoe will be set at well TD (4535 m). In fact, the main
Khami reservoir layers including Lower Gadvan and Lower Fahliyan will be covered by
the 7” liner.

14. Waste Management Program

Detailed Waste management Program  will be prepared by  drilling  fluid service
company and will be delivered separately before rig spud.

1.  While the drilling/completion operation is ongoing, perform all related services
    such as design, supply, execute, supervise and control of waste water, mud and
     drilling cuttings treatment plan complied with Iranian Petroleum Standards (IPS) as
    well as good engineering practices,

2.  Solidification treatment units and/or other proposed methods/units to dispose the
     drilling waste materials (liquids and solids including used drilling and completion
     fluids, drilling cuttings materials, residual acid and sometimes small amounts of oil),
   equipment, Personnel and necessary chemicals for the waste treatment will be
    available

3.  Transport the treated waste materials from the location to the designated area
    specified by Company.

4.  Discharge water after dewatering shall meet below standards:

    ▪  pH=7~9
    ▪   Total hardness< 800 mg/L
    ▪   Turbidity (NTU) < 50
    ▪   T.S.S (Total Suspended Solid) < 80 mg/L
    ▪   Nitrate ion (NO3-) < 50 mg/L
    ▪  Aluminum < 3 mg/L
    ▪  Sulphate < 3200 mg/L
    ▪  Ca2+ < 600 mg/L

5.  Ensure that dried and fixed cuttings can pass the CAN test. For fixing the cuttings,
   100-150 kg cement and 3 - 4-liter sodium silicate shall be used for each cubic meters
    of cuttings.

    Prevent  environmental  pollution,  conserve  resources and  minimize  waste
    materials. Minimize the impact of Work on the environment.

    Appropriate treatment of waste materials shall be undertaken and monitored to
    minimize the impact of discharges and disposals on the environment.

   The waste treatment and disposal shall be performed during the drilling period.

   The system should consist of separation, containment, handling, treatment and

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 185 of 222

    disposal equipment.

 Equipment which should be used to carry out the process are as follow:

    1.  Separation: high efficiency solid and liquid separation system

    2.  Containment and handling

          •  Conveyer: for collection and discharge of all cuttings

          •  Drying  shale  shaker:  giving  dryer  cuttings  and  reduced mud
             consumption

          •  Contingency  tanks:  arrangement  for  the  occasional  collection,
              separation and isolation of cutting wastes

          •   Fluid sump: for the collection of site drains and excess drilling fluid

          •  Tanks: for the storage of excess fluid prior to treatment

          •  Pumps: transfer equipment

          •  Backhoe, dump truck and etc. for the transportation of disposals

    3.  Treatment and disposal:

          •   Fixation: using cement or similar to stabilize the salt or chemicals

          •  Dewatering of excess fluids, site water, and water-based drilling fluid

          •  At least 50 percent of liquid phase should be used in mud making
              system.

    4. Equipment requirement:

          •  Dewatering unit including: dosing pump, PAC and polymer’s tanks

          •  Connectors and hoses for pumps and tanks

          •   Filter press/centrifuge unit

          •  3 buffer tanks including: mixers and transfer pumps

          •  2 air pumps

          •  1 wash down gun

          •  1 submersible pump

          •  Testing equipment such as pH meter, jar test, Hach test, retort kit and
                 titration accessories

15. Health, Safety and Environment

15.1 General  Quality,  Health,  Safety  and  Environmental
    Requirements

COMPANY is committed to conducting operations in a manner which minimizes the
risks to the health and safety of those involved in, and affected by, such operations.
COMPANY is also committed to conducting its operations in a manner which minimizes
the impact on natural and ‘built’ environments in the area of operations.

    1.  This section sets out minimum Quality, Health, Safety and Environmental
       (QHSE) requirements for the WORK that CONTRACTOR and subcontractors will
      be required to comply with whilst conducting operations for COMPANY.

    2. COMPANY may perform audits and assessments of CONTRACTOR’s and its
       subcontractors’ performance under the CONTRACT. COMPANY reserves the
        right without prior notice to conduct quality, health, safety, security and
       environmental audits of the Operations.

    3. CONTRACTOR shall at all times be responsible for performing the WORK in a
      manner so as to protect against loss of life, injury to people, damage to property
      and the environment. The detailed manner means and methods of performing
       the WORK shall be under the control and direction of CONTRACTOR.

    4.  Prior to the start of Operations, certain QHSE processes must be completed.
       These QHSE processes include developing the final QHSE plan format and
       frequency of any QHSE audits, permitting and licensing and COMPANY’s
       approvals. CONTRACTOR shall ensure that sufficient resources are dedicated to
        this task to ensure prior to mobilization.

    5. COMPANY may at any time stop the WORK or any portion of the WORK without
       penalty to COMPANY if the WORK is not conducted in accordance with the
        Quality, Health, Safety or Environmental guidelines specified in this CONTRACT
       or by the laws of the appropriate jurisdiction. COMPANY reserves the right to
       require CONTRACTOR to remove any person from the Operations in the case of
       non-compliance with QHSE procedures. COMPANY may also prohibit the use of
      equipment and plant in cases where that equipment or plant does not meet the

                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03
