       required standards or is deemed unsafe.

    6. CONTRACTOR shall ensure that activities associated with this CONTRACT are
       conducted in compliance with applicable national and local HSE laws and
        regulations, CONTRACTOR’s standards and COMPANY’s requirements and IADC
       documents.
    7.  Limits of Authority: COMPANY’s Representative(s) do not have the authority to
        relax health, safety or environment standards. If they judge the Operation to be
       inadequate with regards to any aspect of quality, health, safety, security or the
       environment, then the operation may be immediately suspended.

QHSE Plan

CONTRACTOR shall be required to develop a project-specific QHSE plan to manage the
WORK and  associated  risks. The  final  project-specific QHSE  plan  shall  satisfy
CONTRACTOR standards, COMPANY requirements and applicable HSE laws and
regulations.

    1.  Contractor should establish an effective HSE system and implement it strictly.
    2.  Contractor must adhere to the standards and regulations of Health, Safe and
       Environment constituted by the local government.
    3.  Contractor must strictly adhere to the procedures / standards and regulations
        of Health, Safety and Environment promulgated by NIOC.
    4.  Contractor should comply with all applicable local government regulations
        including  guidelines and recommended  practices and any other  Iranian
        authorities having jurisdiction.
    5.  All personnel should respect the local culture, traditions and social values of the
       indigenous population of the Iran and Sepehr-Jufair region.
    6.  Contractor  shall  hire  qualified and experienced worker and  familiar  his
       personnel with hazards especially with H2S and Mine and UXO (unexploded
       ordinance) according to related procedure.
     7.  Contractor shall conduct proper training about UXO (unexploded ordinance)
      and mine awareness to all personnel

15.2 Health & Safety Management

    1. CONTRACTOR shall take all necessary safety measures in relation to the WORK
       to be provided and shall conduct itself and its work force in such a way as to
      comply at all times with the provisions of the national and local health and
        safety regulations pertinent to the contracted Scope of WORK.
    2. CONTRACTOR shall also comply with all and any of COMPANY’S safety standards
      and requirements as particularly applicable to the Operating Area and/or as
     may be imposed by COMPANY from time to time.
    3. CONTRACTOR and its subcontractors shall take all necessary steps to provide a
       safe and healthy working environment for employees, COMPANY’s personnel
      and third parties in the performance of the WORK.
    4. CONTRACTOR shall operate a management system that ensures:
       ▪  CONTRACTOR’s and sub contractors’ personnel operate safe systems of
         work at all times for the performance of the WORK.
       ▪  Hazards associated with the work are identified and appropriate actions are
          taken to prevent accidents.
       ▪   Risks to safety and health of personnel involved in the WORK are identified
         and assessed, and any actions and measurements are implemented to
           either eliminate or reduce these risks to a level that is considered as low as
          reasonably practicable, given the nature of the WORK.
       ▪  Personnel are aware of the identified hazards and risks to their safety and
           health and to the safety and health of others.

The detailed safe working procedures established by CONTRACTOR to ensure that the
WORK will be conducted with due regard to the QHSE aspects, shall be detailed or
referenced in the QHSE plan. Copies of the source documents shall be provided to
COMPANY if the procedures are referenced only.

QHSE Representative

CONTRACTOR shall provide a dedicated, on-crew QHSE Representative. The primary
responsibility of the QHSE Representative shall include, but not be limited to, the
maintenance and monitoring of the implementation of HSE guidelines and procedures
and where required provide HSE awareness training for the crew. Such activities shall
be approved and actively supported by CONTRACTOR at corporate management level.

Training and Competence

CONTRACTOR shall ensure that its employees and those of its subcontractors are
trained and competent to perform the WORK in a safe and environmentally responsible
manner. Such training and demonstration of competence will include but not be limited
to vocational, safety, emergency, environmental and other training required by
regulatory agencies and IADC/OGP guidelines. CONTRACTOR’s senior staff shall have
received training in safety management.

Accident/Incident Reporting

CONTRACTOR shall report all incidents. Any hazardous incident / near miss involving
COMPANY, CONTRACTOR’s, or any third-party personnel, plant or equipment, shall be
reported to COMPANY, whether or not injury to personnel or damage/loss to plant or
equipment resulted.

The following incidents shall be verbally reported immediately to the on-site COMPANY
Representative(s): damage to property or equipment belonging to COMPANY, third
parties, CONTRACTOR or its subcontractors; injury to personnel of COMPANY, third
parties, subcontractors or CONTRACTOR; near misses which may have had serious
potential consequences; environmental damage; media attention. An initial written
report, containing factual information only, shall be prepared by CONTRACTOR and
delivered to the COMPANY Representative within twenty-four (24) hours of the
incident.

    •  COMPANY reserves the right to participate in the investigation of any incident
        arising out of performance of the WORK.
    •  CONTRACTOR and  all subcontractors shall have an accident and incident
       reporting system, details of which shall be included in the QHSE plan.
    •  CONTRACTOR shall maintain and forward to COMPANY, a monthly summary of
          its safety performance.
    •  CONTRACTOR shall prepare and forward to COMPANY, a weekly report stating
          all incidents and near misses that occurred during that week.
    •   All CONTRACTOR and subcontractor personnel and COMPANY Representative
        shall attend safety meetings on a regular basis. COMPANY Representative shall
      be given a copy of the minutes of the meetings.

Safety Equipment & Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) requirements
    •  CONTRACTOR  shall ensure  that  appropriate PPE  compliant  to  national
       standards  is supplied to CONTRACTOR’s personnel involved in the WORK.
     COMPANY  intends to use water-based mud and hence CONTRACTOR’s
       Personnel that may be exposed to this must be provided with appropriate PPE.

    •  CONTRACTOR shall be solely liable for all costs in equipping its own and all other
       personnel on contract to, or under the control of, CONTRACTOR with the
       required safety equipment. CONTRACTOR will provide to all CONTRACTOR’s
       Personnel appropriate PPE throughout the WORK. Any defective PPE will be
       replaced without delay. Personnel without the correct PPE will not be allowed
       to continue work, until replacement PPE has been issued. If any person is
       disallowed to work owing to inadequate PPE then COMPANY will deduct his
       day-rate from CONTRACTOR’s invoice.

    •  CONTRACTOR shall detail in the QHSE plan the PPE proposed for issue to the
       crew. This proposal should include the arrangements for replacement (due to
      wear and tear and damage) of this equipment.

Health

This well is committed to develop for oil in a responsible manner that promotes
employees' and local inhabitants' health.

    •  A trained Paramedic and proper MEDIVAC equipment and ambulance shall be
       stationed on the drilling rig during the drilling campaign.
    •  An emergency medical evacuation plan shall be coordinated, and shall keep in
       touch with emergency response centres manned by qualified doctor 24-hours
       per day in Tehran or Ahwaz city.
    •  Medical emergency report and investigation shall be performed as soon as
        possible in accordance with Medical Emergency Procedures.
    •  CONTRACTOR shall, as a minimum, conduct the WORK and provide personnel,
       equipment, facilities and services consistent with good oil field practices and
     OGP  Guidelines. An assessment  of  the  Contract Area  with  regard  to
       “Environmental and Occupational Health Risks” and “Local Medical Support”
        shall be provided by CONTRACTOR in the QHSE plan. This assessment and the
        resulting “Health Risk Assessment Score” will be discussed with COMPANY, and
       a final assessment will be agreed.

Drug Abuse and Control
    •  CONTRACTOR warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel
       to a search of property of such Personnel when COMPANY suspects that such
       Personnel have in their possession drugs and/or other prohibited substances or
      any other item likely to prejudice safety or security. COMPANY will provide a
       representative to attend the search.
    •  CONTRACTOR warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel
       to medical testing for the presence of drugs or other prohibited substances.
    •  CONTRACTOR further agrees that any Personnel who refuse to provide such
       consent or consents or withdraws such consent or consents shall at COMPANY’s
       option be removed from the WORK and shall not, at COMPANY’s option, be
       assigned to any future services to be provided by CONTRACTOR for COMPANY.
    •  CONTRACTOR further agrees that it shall immediately notify COMPANY in the
       event that CONTRACTOR becomes aware that any of its Personnel has been
       found  in  possession  of  drugs and/or any  other  prohibited  substance.
      CONTRACTOR is required to notify COMPANY in the event that any Personnel
       provided for the WORK have previously been found in possession, or have
      abused drugs and/or other substances prohibited at Location. The acceptance
        of such Personnel for the WORK shall be at the sole discretion of COMPANY.

Emergency Preparedness

CONTRACTOR and its subcontractors shall have emergency preparedness and response
plans plus procedures available at all times throughout the duration of the CONTRACT.

CONTRACTOR shall take all necessary measures to ensure that:

    A.  Potential emergency situations have been identified and assessed;
    B. An emergency response plan, including clear responsibilities, is established;
    C. CONTRACTOR’s management, employees and third parties are aware of
       response procedures and responsibilities;
    D. Regular drills and exercises are carried out to test the emergency response
       organization and procedures and that any resultant issues are properly
       addressed.

CONTRACTOR shall notify COMPANY immediately with respect to any emergency
situation and shall provide regular updates on emergency planning procedures, tests etc.

CONTRACTOR shall detail in the QHSE plan a list of potential contingencies for which
emergency response procedures are planned. The QHSE plan should also detail a
schedule of proposed exercises and drills to verify the effectiveness of the proposed
arrangements in place. Development and implementation of the above plan shall
incorporate any COMPANY assigned personnel to the project.

▪  Requirement on location
    1. The access road and well location construction shall be accorded with

       contamination, and prevent all surface water contamination.

      and tools shall be carried out strictly to avoid damage farm and trees, etc.
    3.  Well site shall be flat and clean. All the equipment, materials, drilling tools
        shall be put in order.
    4.  Control the rig noise to decrease the influence to surroundings while drilling at
       the inhabitation area.
    5.  Contractor shall perform on site segregation of wastes prior to transfer to the
        transfer area and/or delivery to the transportation Contractor in accordance
       with the following waste hazard classification.

▪  Non-Hazardous Wastes (Re-usable, Recyclable and Recoverable Wastes)
    1)  Ferrous and non-ferrous metals, plastics, and wood wastes, PETs and

        potentially paper and glass

    2)  Materials shall be separately segregated and provision may be made for size

       reduction and containerization equipment.

    3) The Contractor/Generators are encouraged to seek for recycle/re-use

        alternatives based on the Local available facilities, for final disposal of these

        materials.

    4)  It should be noted that landfill disposal of metal waste materials and wood

       wastes shall be prohibited. For wood wastes re-use of and/or salvage of

       commercial timber, or deliver/release to

    5)  Local community are recommended.

▪  Hazardous Wastes

Hazardous Waste, such as  clinic waste,  oil waste, waste produced by painting,
insulation works shall be duly segregated.

▪   Facilities of environment protection supplied to well site
    1) Mud purifying circulation system

▪  Requirement of environment protection on location

 Sewage pit Area of the sewage pit: 1500 m2

    1)  Prior to spud mud pit and sewage pit must comply with the standard. Install

        circulating system for cleaning rig floor and equipment.
    2)  Install BOP stack and pressure test shall be operated according to the drilling
       design to avoid blowout.
    3)  Install oil supply lines and check whether there is any leakage.

▪  Management of environment protection on location
    1)  In drilling operation ，keep mud and sewage from overflowing. If it happens,
       clean up timely.
    2) Mud chemicals shall be stored according to the standard and shall not be
       scattered around well site. If mud chemicals scatter while loading or
       unloading, clean it up at once. The sack of mud chemicals shall be recovered.
     Do not dispose it on site.
    3) Enhance water management at well location and do not wash equipment with
        fresh water. Stop water flowing for long time. Overhead tanks shall be utilized.
    4)  Oil management shall be carefully at the well site. No oil dirt on site, no
       dripping and leaking at oil tank and manifold system area.
    5)  After the well is completed, reinforce the sewage pit ，solidify the disposed
     mud and have environment procedure fulfilled.
    6)  Install fuel supply lines and keep them in good condition.
    7)  All the drilling fluid materials shall be recovered and shall not be left at the
       well site.
    8) The environmental protection personnel from contractor and operator side
        shall check and accept the well site environment according to the regulation

          waste materials (e.g. acids, and alkalis, organic materials and strong

            oxidizing agents).
    ➢  Capable enough to provide storage for the entire range of hazardous

          wastes generated during the work.
    ➢ The area shall be provided with Spill Containment and means of

           preventing run off to and from the area.
    ➢  All waste containers to be stored shall be appropriately labeled.
    ➢ Hazardous Waste shall be transferred in a timely manner, within a

        maximum period of 48 hours, for any period in excess of this permission

            shall be granted from Company.

▪  Standard of utilization and discharging of wastes on location
    1) Conduct drilling operation according to the design, drilling mud is needed
       before spud.
    2) Sewage that cannot be recovered shall be put into the pit, do not discharge. If
       the volume of sewage is too big and some should be discharged, the quality of
       drainage must comply with the following regulations.

1) Disposed oil shall be recovered entirely. Recovery rate shall be 100％. Dumping of
   waste oil shall be prohibited.

2)  In drilling operation, non-chrome thinner high temperature mud system shall be
   used often if engineering is qualified.

3) While drilling in special area adopt mud creation inhabitation techniques to
    decrease the waste mud creation.

4)  After Suspension, all the mud chemicals, bentonite, clays shall be recovered and
    shall not be left at the well site. Mud in purifying tank shall also be recovered after
    Suspension.

5)  In case of any flammable or noxious gas encountered, steps shall be taken at once
    to prevent kicks and blowout. Gas shall be guided into ignition equipment to burn
      it away.

6)  Burning material that may result in serious smog or horrible smell at the well site is
    not allowed. For cement or barite that may result in granular dust pollution,
    operation shall be performed in a closed system.

7)  For abandoned well filled with cement plugs to seal the oil, gas or water formation.

   by the Company.

    party Sub-Contractor of the Contractor/Generator itself). The Waste Transporter

15.6 Meetings

In order to clarify COMPANY’S QHSE expectations of CONTRACTOR and to develop the
QHSE plan for the WORK the following meeting shall be held:

▪   Initial QHSE Management Meeting

attended by COMPANY and CONTRACTOR Representatives and QHSE Managers.
During this meeting, the QHSE plan will be discussed, specific action items identified,
and responsibilities for follow-up determined. At COMPANY’S discretion this meeting
may be made part of the QHSE Planning Meeting.

▪  QHSE Planning Meeting

attended by CONTRACTOR’s Representative and QHSE Managers, as proposed.
Scheduled after the  Initial QHSE Management Meeting and before the start of
operations; at this meeting CONTRACTOR  will provide a specific QHSE plan for
COMPANY’S review.

The outcome of the meeting will be a list of action items, time for suspension, and
responsibilities for suspension and acceptance. COMPANY will review the information
presented and will respond in a timely manner. Under no circumstances will the WORK
start before the specific QHSE plan has been approved by COMPANY.

▪   Site Start-Up Meeting(s)

attended by all field personnel including subcontractor personnel and COMPANY’S field
representatives. The purposes of the meeting(s) will be to:

    a.  Inform personnel of COMPANY’s and CONTRACTOR’s QHSE requirements and
       procedures;
    b.  Inform personnel of specific HSE hazards, mitigating measures and procedures
       to be followed;
     c.  Inform personnel, including subcontractor supervisors and personnel of their
      QHSE responsibilities;
    d. Review emergency response and contingency plans.

15.8 Well Control

(1) Recording Reference

All BOP tests, drills, function tests, any malfunctions, repair or maintenance to the mud
system and well control equipment shall be recorded in the IADC daily reports and shall
be signed by both the Drilling Contractor's Tool Pusher and Drilling Supervisor on the
well site. They shall also be recorded in the Daily Drilling Report.

(2) Flow Check Procedure

To avoid a kick situation, it is recommended that a flow check be conducted at specific
times during the drilling operation.

       •  While drilling (any warning sign)
       •   Prior to tripping the drill string
       •  While tripping (any warning sign)
       •  While out of hole(continuously)
       •   Trip out:

       ➢  After pulling 5% of the drill pipe

       ➢  At the midpoint depth of the wellbore

       ➢  Prior to pulling the first stand of drill collars

       ➢  After all the drill string is out of hole.

       •   Trip in:

       ➢  Upon reaching surface casing shoe

       ➢  At midpoint of the wellbore

(3) Primary Control Reference

It is the responsibility of the Drilling Contractor to keep the hole full at all times by using
all the available equipment. The Drilling Supervisor shall monitor that the correct
preventive practices are being implemented and/or conducted at all times.

Extreme care shall be taken to monitor mud volume, drilling breaks and gas cut mud.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 205 of 222

The MACP (Maximum allowable casing pressure) shall be confirmed by the Drilling
Supervisor, either after setting each new casing string or, whenever the density of the
drilling mud changes.

The MACP shall be clearly written on a kick control sheet that will be posted near the
choke control panel.

The predetermined distance at which the Tool Joint is to be pulled above the rotary
should be posted on rig floor near the BOP control panel.

The Driller  is responsible for carefully measuring and recording the slow pump
pressure. Awareness of these values is an important element in killing operations, in
order to avoid formation breakdown.

Take a measure and record the slow pump pressure at three different flow rates with
all pumps at the following times:

         1)  Once per tour.

         2)  Whenever changes occur in the mud density/rheology.

         3)  Whenever changes occur in the dimension and characteristics of the
               string, BHA, jet size, jet plugged or jet lost, etc.

         4)  Secondary Control Reference

          Any time a drilling break  is noticed, drilling shall stop and a static
            monitoring of the well shall be carried out. Space-out for all Pipe Rams
           and Annular Preventer must be known to the Driller at all times to keep
             tool joints clear of annular preventer and rams.

         5)  Kick Detection Procedure While Drilling

                    If there is an indication of a possible kick occurring, the Driller shall follow
            the kick detection procedures outlined below:
            Stop drilling with the pump on→Pick up top drive to a predetermined
            space-out position→Shut off the pumps and check for well flow by visual
            examination on the bell nipple or flow line.

             If the well is not flowing:

       •  Immediately notify the rig manager, Drilling Supervisor and Tool pusher.

       •  The Drilling Supervisor will decide whether to circulate bottoms up or
          resume drilling. The penetration rate will be limited as long as the cause of
           the abnormal situation  is undetermined. Always be aware that some

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 206 of 222

           hydrocarbons (gas or oil cut mud) may rise to the surface. Ensure that the
           proper monitoring and safety systems all in place.

             If the well is flowing:

       •  Close the well as per the Shut-in Procedure.

       •  Immediately notify the Wellsite Drilling Supervisor, rig manager and Tool
           pusher.

(4)  Soft Shut-in Procedure and Line Up

During operations the circuit has to be lined up as follows:

       •  Manuel valve on choke line is opened

       •  Hydraulic valve on choke line has to be closed

       •  The choke on choke manifold has to be opened

       •   All the valves leading to the separator passing by the choke (downstream
            valves) have to be in open positions.

       •  Beside these valves, all the valves have to be in closed position.

    ▪  Soft Shut in procedure while drilling

      The procedure is performed as follows:

              -    if there is any kick indication, stop rotation, pick up off bottom and space
             out (Tool joint one meter above rotary table to avoid getting the next
               tool joint on the pipe rams)
              -   Stop pumps, perform flow check. If the well is flowing then:
              -  Open choke line valve at the BOP stack (Called HCR)
              -   Close annular BOP
              -   Close Choke
              -  Read and Record pressures and times. Check pit volume gain in order to
             prepare the kill sheet
            -    If the gas is migrating, control the wellbore pressures during the shut in.

     ▪   Soft Shut in procedure while tripping

             If there is any kick indication, stop tripping immediately. Here, two different

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 207 of 222

         situations can be identified:

         a) The well is flowing, then the shut in is proceeded as follows:

                -   Set the string on the slips
                -   Install a fully opening safety valve in open position. Close the valve
              once is installed
                -  Open choke line valve at BOP stack (HCR valve)
                -   Close annular BOP
                -   Close choke.
                -  Read and record pressures and times, check pit volumes

             It depends on the situation to weather to start killing procedures or to strip
        back to the bottom.

                -    If the stripping is faced, then, Stab IBOP (Grey valve)
                -  Open the fully opening safety valve
                -  Reduce the annular pressure and start stripping the string to the
             bottom

         b) The well is not flowing:

                -   Set the string on the slips
                -   Install IBOP (grey valve or non-return valve)
                -  Run back in the hole with controlling the volumes, if any anomalies are
               noticed then proceed to the stripping. Once on bottom, circulate
               annular volume and evaluate the situation.

15.9 H2S Monitoring and Prevention Measure

Monitoring H2S

1) Somewhere on the well site is apt to accumulate H2S, such as mud pit, shale shaker,
   and drilling platform, should be installed one set of. Make sure H2S monitor and
    alarm system can be used.
2) Alarm threshold setting for H2S warning indicator: the  first warning level  is
   10mg/m3, unable warning sound, just show present value of H2S content to
    operators. The second warning level is 20mg/m3, when the H2S content in air
   exceed 20mg/m3, H2S warning indicator can give an alarm automatically, and
   warning sound should be heard by everyone work in well site. The audible alarm
    should be installed on Kelly board.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 208 of 222

3) Equip enough gas masks on drilling platform, shaker screen (total amount should
    large than 24), and equip 12 sets of 30min positive pressure Air Aspirators，2 sets
    of portable H2S monitor and appropriative H2S warning indicator. Arrange a person
    specially assigned for taking charge all the protective apparatus. This man must do
    periodical check to keep this equipment in a good condition.
4) H2S monitor should be done weekly check and compulsory examination.
5)  After drilling into the pay zone, strengthen the detection of H2S content in mud.
6)  Contractor shall implement H2S service system by himself or 3rd party for
    controlling the risk of H2S leakage.
7)  All equipment and  devices  shall be on  location before Tarbur  formation
    penetration, service Personnel shall be on location before Spud in the hole section
   which contains H2S or other poisonous gases.

Measures for H2S prevention

1) Windsocks shall be set on the drill floor and in the monsoon direction or/and
    prevailing wind direction of Sepehr-Jufair oil field, off well site. In emergency,
   employees must follow the evacuation procedure.
2)  Explosion-proof blowers shall be installed around the rig, shale shaker, and drilling
    fluid tanks to blow away toxic and flammable gas at the well site.
3) The rig crew in the H2S area shall be furnished with H2S monitors and adequate
    protective apparatus in accordance with IPS. Everybody shall be able to use,
    maintain, and check the apparatus.
4) The technicians of rig crew are responsible for H2S proof training and the tool
   pusher is in charge of the supervision and examination.
5) The pH value of drilling fluid in the H2S area shall be kept over 9.5. Pay attention to
   measurement of H2S concentration in drilling fluid. The H2S concentration shall be
    kept at less than 50mg/cm3 by using sulphur removal agents and degassers.
6)  Personnel safety and protection shall be carried out according to IPS during
    operations in polluted areas where H2S content exceeds the critical concentration.
7) When the rig crew makes decision to bleed and fire because well control cannot be
   performed due to well site conditions, operations shall be performed according to
    procedures in IPS.
8) Once blowout occurs, the incident shall be reported to the higher-level department
    as soon as possible and firefighting trucks, ambulances, medical care personnel and
    technicians shall be on duty at the well site.
9)  After the blowout is controlled, H2S concentration should be measured in the

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and
                      Production Operations Project
                     Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No:               SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03         Page 209 of 222

    places where H2S is possible to gather.
10) The personnel are not allowed to access until the H2S concentration is below the
     critical concentration.

The H2S system equipment provided shall be designed for the safety-zone-area
classification where they are deployed. Equipment supplied by the service company for
the purpose of the work shall at all times be maintained in good conditions of the
performance of the services.
